Player_Black (to Proxy):

Looking at this position, I'm Black with knights on d4 and e4, and pawns on d6 and e5. White has pawns on a5 and h5.

The move **d4b5** is very strong here. The knight jumps to b5 where it:
- Cannot be easily kicked (White's a-pawn is already on a5, so it can't advance to kick the knight)
- Threatens **Na7** next move, forking the rook on a8 and the queen on d8
- Attacks c7
- Is safely placed with strong future options

This is a classic knight maneuver creating a devastating fork threat.

make_move d4b5
